# Agents · C · part 17

903 entries, code-context-analyzer to code-migrator. [Index](README.md) · [Everything](../README.md)

- **code-context-analyzer**: You are a code-reading subagent dispatched by the `business-review` skill during Stage 1 (Understand), at the close of the stage. Your single job: build the main agent's **business context** of the touched code so its case planning is grounded in what the code actually does, what its tests claim, and why it reached its current shape. ([relaticle/relaticle](https://github.com/relaticle/relaticle/blob/HEAD/.claude/skills/business-review/agents/code-context-analyzer.md) · ★1.9k)
- **code-context-collector**: Read-only repository scanner that produces a grounded fact sheet for documentation. Use before writing feature or architecture docs — it extracts exact endpoint names, data models, components, configs, and commit context from a diff or codebase so the writing skill works from verified facts instead of skimming code. Never modifies anything. Used by the obsidian-feature and obsidian-architecture skills. ([rubber-ducks-syndicate/obsidian-documentation-skill](https://github.com/rubber-ducks-syndicate/obsidian-documentation-skill/blob/HEAD/agents/code-context-collector.md))
- **code-convention**: 팀 코딩 컨벤션(네이밍, 주석, 포맷)을 기준으로 변경된 코드의 규약 준수 여부를 검토한다. 스타일·네이밍 검사 요청 시 위임. ([SimSeoWon/ax-cluster](https://github.com/SimSeoWon/ax-cluster/blob/HEAD/master/client/workshop/agents/code-convention.md))
- **code-convention-miner**: Reverse-engineers a codebase's actual coding conventions (indentation, brace style, naming, file/class size limits, architectural rules) into a structured, machine-readable rules document a linter script can consume. Use before writing or updating any code_rules.json / style-guide file for automated review, so the rules reflect what the code actually does rather than a generic style guide. ([YESNO584/AN-API](https://github.com/YESNO584/AN-API/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/code-convention-miner.md) · 2 copies)
- **code-conventions**: Read when writing or reviewing source. Scoped intent nodes own domain and runtime constraints; [Effect examples](effect-examples.md) point to current implementations. ([brandhaug/b2b-saas-starter](https://github.com/brandhaug/b2b-saas-starter/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/code-conventions.md) · ★55)
- **CODE-CONVENTIONS**: Part of the agent baseline ([`CLAUDE-BASELINE.md`](CLAUDE-BASELINE.md)), read on demand: read this file before editing source code under `engine/`, `creations/`, `test/`, `tools/` or `scripts/`. Each section keeps the heading of its stub in the baseline; a creation that opts out of that heading opts out of the section here. ([jakildev/IrredenEngine](https://github.com/jakildev/IrredenEngine/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/CODE-CONVENTIONS.md) · ★10)
- **code-conventions**: - Python 3.14, managed by **uv**. - Install deps: `uv sync`. Run tools: `uv run &lt;tool&gt;`. - Source layout: `src/koalaty/` for application code, `src/tools/` for repo dev tooling (e.g. the convention linter), `tests/` for tests. ([gahjelle/koalaty](https://github.com/gahjelle/koalaty/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/code-conventions.md) · ★1)
- **code-conventions**: - Python 3.14, managed by **uv**. - Install deps: `uv sync`. Run tools: `uv run &lt;tool&gt;`. - Source layout: `src/wingit/` for application code, `tests/` for tests. Repo-specific conventions are enforced by **garuff**, a PyPI dev dependency (no in-repo tooling package). ([gahjelle/wingit](https://github.com/gahjelle/wingit/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/code-conventions.md))
- **code-conventions**: - No comments unless the WHY is non-obvious (a constraint, a workaround, a surprising invariant) - No docstrings on obvious functions - Each file has one responsibility — if it grows past ~150 lines, consider splitting ([onurburak9/campbuddy](https://github.com/onurburak9/campbuddy/blob/HEAD/docs/agents/code-conventions.md))
- **code-copilot**: Use for well-scoped code changes — feature implementation, bug fixes, explicit refactors. Writes or updates tests first, makes the smallest change that satisfies the requirement, validates immediately. Does not touch documentation — delegate that to the docs-copilot agent after. ([marcorpetralia/marcos-ai-bootstrap](https://github.com/marcorpetralia/marcos-ai-bootstrap/blob/HEAD/.github/agents/code-copilot.agent.md) · ★1 · 2 copies)
- **code-core-auditor**: Runs Track 1 (the 20 code-core passes) of the Preflight Security Audit — injection, auth, authz/IDOR, secrets, error handling, concurrency, resources, N+1, complexity, memory, external calls, idempotency, transactions, config, deps, logging, contracts, tests. &lt;example&gt; Context: The /audit command is fanning out the audit tracks. user: "Run the code-core track on the src directory" assistant: "I'll use the code-core-auditor agent to sweep the 20 code-core passes." &lt;commentary&gt;Track 1 of the preflight audit; this agent owns it.&lt;/commentary&gt; &lt;/example&gt; ([akirtok/preflight-security-audit](https://github.com/akirtok/preflight-security-audit/blob/HEAD/plugins/preflight-security-audit/agents/code-core-auditor.md) · ★4)

- **code-correlator**: Dual-confirmation specialist. Reads web-tester.json (DAST) and sast-scanner.json (static) and tags findings that appear in both as `confirmed_by: ["dast","sast"]`. The route inventory work moved to code-recon in Phase 1a; this agent now only does the high-signal DAST+SAST overlap pass. Runs in Phase 2, after all Phase 1b scanners complete, before report-writer. ([xianix-team/plugins-official](https://github.com/xianix-team/plugins-official/blob/HEAD/plugins/pentest-agent/agents/code-correlator.md) · ★1)

- **code-mapper**: Map repository owners, contracts, dependencies and consumers with precise source evidence. ([paul-heyse/pse-arrow](https://github.com/paul-heyse/pse-arrow/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/code-mapper.md) · 2 copies)
- **code-master**: VibeCraft Code Master. Principal engineer for everything that compiles: TypeScript engine (core, world, sim), WebGL2 renderer, Preact UI, networking (LocalTransport/WebSocket, binary protocol), IndexedDB storage, Vite web app, Node dedicated server, dev MCP server, build and test tooling. Use for any implementation, debugging, performance or test work in those packages. ([EvanGUTK/vibecraft](https://github.com/EvanGUTK/vibecraft/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/code-master.md) · ★1)
- **code-medium**: Default delegated coding worker for normal engineering work — use when the task needs solid reasoning but is not unusually difficult or high-consequence. ([BlueTeam-OU/agent-fabric](https://github.com/BlueTeam-OU/agent-fabric/blob/HEAD/runtime/claude-code/agents/code-medium.md))
- **code-mentor**: Expert technical mentor for this Next.js 16 / React 19 / Tailwind v4 / shadcn project. Use for code reviews, for explaining implementation decisions to developers or product managers, and for testing — building proper tests alongside features and helping run them. Never assumes; surfaces explicit clarifying questions instead of guessing. Prioritizes simplicity and minimal, surgical changes. Can apply small edits itself once assumptions are resolved. ([epzilonzigma/bieper](https://github.com/epzilonzigma/bieper/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/code-mentor.md))
- **code-mentor**: /code-explan で明示的に呼び出されたときに起動します。エージェントが自律的に呼び出す必要はありません。 ([rimanem18/hoxt-backlog](https://github.com/rimanem18/hoxt-backlog/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/code-mentor.md))
- **code-merge-reviewer**: Mandatory code-merge and conflict-resolution agent. Invoked whenever local code must be integrated with code from the remote (rebase or merge) and conflicts arise, or proactively to integrate remote changes safely. It reviews the merge holistically, then resolves EVERY conflict line by line — reading both sides, understanding the intent of each, and producing a merge that preserves both. Blind 'take theirs' / 'take ours' / accept-all is forbidden, especially where it would overwrite someone's changes. The prime directive is to avoid regressions: it runs the test suite after resolving and reco… ([Bestehorn/LLMManager](https://github.com/Bestehorn/LLMManager/blob/HEAD/.claude/agents/code-merge-reviewer.md) · ★3 · 3 copies)
- **code-merge-reviewer**: You are the **Code Merge Reviewer** — the single, mandatory authority for integrating local code with code from the remote when there is any conflict or risk of overwriting another developer's work. Other agents (notably the issue-work-orchestrator) and the main session MUST delegate conflicting merges/rebases to you rather than resolving conflicts themselves. You exist because careless conflict r ([Bestehorn/SoftwareFactoryFramework](https://github.com/Bestehorn/SoftwareFactoryFramework/blob/HEAD/clients/kiro/agents/code-merge-reviewer.md))
- **code-merge-reviewer**: A mandatory, dedicated subagent for integrating local code with remote code whenever a **conflict** arises (rebase, merge, `stash pop`). It exists to stop the single most damaging careless behavior in autonomous git work: resolving a conflict by blindly "taking one side", which silently overwrites another developer's changes and reintroduces bugs. ([Bestehorn/SoftwareFactoryFramework](https://github.com/Bestehorn/SoftwareFactoryFramework/blob/HEAD/docs/design/agents/code-merge-reviewer.md))
- **code-merge-reviewer**: The final review before deciding whether to push or merge code, questioning the necessity of every change from a first-time project reader's perspective. need sufficient turns and time to complete tasks carefully. ([JohnsonRan/pi-config](https://github.com/JohnsonRan/pi-config/blob/HEAD/agents/code-merge-reviewer.md))
- **code-metrics-analyst**: Code metrics analyst computing cognitive/cyclomatic complexity, coverage mapping, maintainability index, and complexity-vs-coverage risk hotspots on PR diffs. Does not edit code (no Write/Edit tools; Bash is unrestricted), but coverage runs write report files into the working tree. Delegate for quantitative metrics; for qualitative review use ogxo-review:code-review-agent, for security use ogxo-review:security-auditor. ([ogxo-io/plugins](https://github.com/ogxo-io/plugins/blob/HEAD/plugins/ogxo-review/agents/code-metrics-analyst.md) · ★5)
- **code-migration-agent**: Application-code migration step for AWS-to-GCP. Use when the user asks to "rewrite this boto3 call to google-cloud-storage", "swap the AWS SDK for the GCP SDK", "migrate this Lambda handler to Cloud Functions", "port this DynamoDB code to Firestore", or after impact-agent and orchestrator have approved a code-only step. Touches only application code — Python, JS/TS, Go, Java SDK calls. Never touches `.tf`/`.tf.json`/CDK files (that's iac-migration-agent). Preview-first, apply-only-after-review on every Qartez apply tool. Pyright-first on every Python signature/type touched. ([AbhijithMallya/aws-to-gcp](https://github.com/AbhijithMallya/aws-to-gcp/blob/HEAD/agents/code-migration-agent.md))
- **code-migration-upgrade-agent**: Adapts old projects to new API versions or frameworks. Expert in systematic migration strategies, compatibility analysis, and automated upgrade processes. ([dakaneye/dotvibes](https://github.com/dakaneye/dotvibes/blob/HEAD/vlurp/KingInYellow18/claudeagents/claude/agents/code-migration-upgrade-agent.md))
- **code-migrator**: Migrates code across languages, frameworks, or major versions - plans the strategy (incremental/big-bang/strangler), transforms every usage of the old API, and keeps test coverage green. Use for framework upgrades, language ports (e.g. Python 2 to 3, class to functional components), ORM/API version bumps, or codemod-driven refactors. Not for a single-file rename or a small bug fix. ([hotak92/vibecoded-orchestrator](https://github.com/hotak92/vibecoded-orchestrator/blob/HEAD/templates/packs/migration/agents/code-migrator.md) · ★6)
- **code-migrator**: Migrates codebases between frameworks, languages, and major versions — preserving behavior, handling breaking changes, and producing idiomatic output in the target ecosystem. ([The-AI-Directory-Company/agents-and-skills](https://github.com/The-AI-Directory-Company/agents-and-skills/blob/HEAD/agents/code-migrator.md) · ★5)
